From Wikipedia, the free encyclopedia

American Mexican restaurant chain

This article is about the restaurant chain. For the food item, see [Chipotle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle "Chipotle").

**Chipotle Mexican Grill, Inc.** ([/tʃɪˈpoʊtleɪ/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [_chih-POAT-lay_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key")),[\[4\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-barnes-4) also known simply as **Chipotle**, is an American multinational chain of [fast casual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fast_casual "Fast casual") restaurants specializing in bowls, [tacos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taco "Taco"), and [Mission burritos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_burrito "Mission burrito") made to order in front of the customer.[\[5\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-5)[\[6\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-6)[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-lexpress-2012may31-7) As of December 2025, Chipotle has 4,000 locations.[\[8\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-8) Its name derives from _[chipotle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle "Chipotle")_, the [Nahuatl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahuatl "Nahuatl") name (from _chilpoctli_) for a smoked and dried [jalapeño](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalape%C3%B1o "Jalapeño") [chili pepper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chili_pepper "Chili pepper").[\[9\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-9)[\[10\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-rocky2-founder-10)

Chipotle was one of the first chains of [fast casual restaurants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fast_casual_restaurant "Fast casual restaurant").[\[11\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-11) It was founded by [Steve Ells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Ells "Steve Ells") on July 13, 1993. Ells was the founder, chairman, and CEO of Chipotle. He was inspired to open the restaurant after visiting taquerias and burrito shops in San Francisco's [Mission District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_District "Mission District") while working as a chef. Ells wanted to show customers that fresh ingredients could be used to quickly serve food. Chipotle had 16 restaurants (all in Colorado) when [McDonald's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonald's "McDonald's") became a major investor in 1998. By the time McDonald's fully divested itself from Chipotle in 2006, the chain had grown to over 500 locations.[\[12\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-rocky-12) With more than 2,000 locations, Chipotle had a net income of US$475.6million and a staff of more than 45,000 employees in 2015.[\[13\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-cmgearnings2015-13)

By 2025, Chipotle had restaurants in 48 states and the District of Columbia but not in the states of Alaska or Hawaii [\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-14)[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-15)[\[16\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-16) nor in the American overseas territories of Puerto Rico, the Virgin Islands, American Samoa, or Guam.

## History

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=1 "Edit section: History")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/University_of_Denver_campus_pics_107.jpg/250px-University_of_Denver_campus_pics_107.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:University_of_Denver_campus_pics_107.jpg) The first Chipotle, near the campus of the [University of Denver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Denver "University of Denver")

[Steve Ells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Ells "Steve Ells") attended [the Culinary Institute of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Culinary_Institute_of_America "The Culinary Institute of America") in [Hyde Park, New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyde_Park,_New_York "Hyde Park, New York"). Afterward, he became a line cook for [Jeremiah Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeremiah_Tower "Jeremiah Tower") at [Stars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stars_(restaurant) "Stars (restaurant)") in San Francisco.[\[17\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-parrish-17) There, Ells observed the popularity of the [taquerías](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taquer%C3%ADa "Taquería") and [San Francisco burritos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_burrito "Mission burrito") in the [Mission District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_District,_San_Francisco "Mission District, San Francisco"). In 1993, Ells took what he learned in San Francisco[\[18\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-18) and opened the first Chipotle Mexican Grill in [Denver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver "Denver"), Colorado, in a former [Dolly Madison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolly_Madison "Dolly Madison") Ice Cream store at 1644 East Evans Avenue,[\[19\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-19) near the [University of Denver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Denver "University of Denver") campus, using an $85,000 loan from his father.[\[12\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-rocky-12) Ells and his father calculated that the store would need to sell 107 burritos per day to be profitable. After one month, the original restaurant was selling over 1,000 burritos a day.[\[10\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-rocky2-founder-10) The second store opened in 1995 using Chipotle's [cash flow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cash_flow "Cash flow"), and the third was opened using an [SBA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_Business_Administration "Small Business Administration") loan. To fund additional growth, Ells' father invested $1.5million. Afterwards, Ells created a [board of directors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Board_of_directors "Board of directors") and business plan, raising an additional $1.8million for the company.[\[20\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-obsession-20) Ells had originally planned to use funds from the first Chipotle to open a fine-dining restaurant, but instead focused on Chipotle Mexican Grill when the restaurants saw success.[\[21\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-Wall-21)[\[22\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-22)

In 1998, the first restaurant outside of Colorado opened in [Kansas City, Missouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas_City,_Missouri "Kansas City, Missouri").[\[23\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-23)

In 1998, [McDonald's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonald's "McDonald's") made an initial minority investment in the company. By 2001, McDonald's had grown to be Chipotle's largest investor.[\[12\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-rocky-12) The investment from McDonald's allowed the firm to quickly expand, from 16 restaurants in 1998 to over 500 by 2005.[\[24\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-24) On January 26, 2006, Chipotle made its [initial public offering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Initial_public_offering "Initial public offering") (IPO) after increasing the share price twice due to high pre-IPO demand. At the time, Chipotle had 500 stores.[\[25\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-:0-25) In its first day as a public company, the stock rose exactly 100%, resulting in the best U.S.-based IPO in six years, and the second-best IPO for a restaurant after [Boston Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Market "Boston Market"). The money from the offering was then used to fund new store growth.[\[26\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-rocky2-feeding-26)

In March 2005, Monty Moran was appointed president and chief operating officer of Chipotle while Ells remained chairman and CEO.[\[27\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-bw-2006dec15-27)

In October 2006, McDonald's fully divested from Chipotle.[\[28\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-buzz-28) This was part of a larger initiative for McDonald's to divest all of its non-core business restaurants—Chipotle, [Donatos Pizza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donatos_Pizza "Donatos Pizza"), and Boston Market—so that it could focus on the main McDonald's chain.[\[29\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-29) McDonald's had invested approximately $360million into Chipotle, and took out $1.5billion.[\[20\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-obsession-20) McDonald's had attempted to get Chipotle to add drive-through windows and a breakfast menu, which Ells resisted.[\[30\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-bn-2015feb02-30)[\[31\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-tmf-2015dec27-31) In 2008, Chipotle opened its first location outside of the United States in [Toronto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toronto "Toronto").[\[32\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-ts-2008aug13-32)

In January 2009, president and chief operating officer Monty Moran was promoted to co-CEO, a position that he would share with Ells, while Moran retained his president position.[\[33\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-reuters-2009jan05-33)

In a list of fastest-growing [restaurant chains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Restaurant_chains "Restaurant chains") in 2009, Chipotle was ranked eighth, based on increases in U.S. sales over the past year,[\[34\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-34) and in 2010 Chipotle was ranked third.[\[35\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-35) _[Consumer Reports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consumer_Reports "Consumer Reports")_ ranked Chipotle as the best Mexican fast-food chain in 2011.[\[36\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-36) The company serves approximately 750,000 customers per day.[\[37\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-37)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill_%2848069356197%29.jpg/250px-Chipotle_Mexican_Grill_%2848069356197%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Chipotle_Mexican_Grill_(48069356197).jpg) Chipotle Mexican Grill

In December 2010, Chipotle hired chef Nate Appleman to develop new cuisine. Appleman has won [Rising Star Chef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Beard_Foundation_Award "James Beard Foundation Award") from the [James Beard Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Beard_Foundation "James Beard Foundation"), was named "Best New Chef" by the [Food & Wine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_&_Wine "Food & Wine") magazine, and competed on _[The Next Iron Chef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Next_Iron_Chef "The Next Iron Chef")_.[\[38\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-38)

In 2010, [U.S. Immigration and Customs Enforcement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Immigration_and_Customs_Enforcement "U.S. Immigration and Customs Enforcement") (ICE) audited Chipotle's Minneapolis restaurants, and found that some employees had been hired using fraudulent documents. In December, Chipotle fired 450 employees from its Minneapolis restaurants as a result of the audit, resulting in protests by local groups.[\[39\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-39)[\[40\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-40) In February 2011, ICE expanded the audit to include 60 restaurants in [Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia "Virginia") and Washington, D.C.[\[41\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-41) which resulted in 40 workers being fired. In April 2011, the criminal division of the [attorney general's office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Attorney_General "United States Attorney General") in Washington, D.C., joined the case, and ICE agents began interviewing employees at 20 to 25 restaurants in other locations, such as Los Angeles and [Atlanta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlanta "Atlanta").[\[42\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-42) In response to the government investigations, Chipotle hired former director of ICE [Julie Myers Wood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julie_Myers "Julie Myers") and high-profile attorneys [Robert Luskin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Luskin "Robert Luskin") and [Gregory B. Craig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregory_B._Craig "Gregory B. Craig").[\[43\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-43)

In April 2014, Chipotle announced an increase in menu prices for the first time in nearly three years, due to increasing costs for steak, avocados, and cheese. The price increase was expected to be rolled out from the end of the second quarter of 2014 through the end of the third quarter.[\[44\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-44) In late 2015, Chipotle expanded its mobile strategy through delivery partnerships with tech startups like [Tapingo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tapingo "Tapingo"), a delivery service that targets college campuses.[\[45\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-45)

In December 2016, Chipotle announced that co-CEO Monty Moran had stepped down from his role effective immediately with Ells becoming the sole CEO.[\[46\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-bi-2016dec12-46)[\[47\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-tmf-2016dec14-47) Eleven months later, Ells announced in November 2017 that he would be stepping down as CEO.[\[48\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-ReplaceFounder-48)

In December 2017, Chipotle announced it signed a 15-year lease and in late 2018 will move around 450 corporate employees—currently housed in multiple buildings around downtown Denver—into the new 1144 Fifteenth Tower and occupy around 126,000 square feet, or 5 floors, of the 40-story tower.[\[49\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-49)

In February 2018, Chipotle announced that Taco Bell CEO [Brian Niccol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Niccol "Brian Niccol") would replace Ells as CEO starting on March 5 while Ells would retain his chairman position.[\[50\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-nyt-2018feb13-50) Many industry analysts praised Niccol's appointment saying that Chipotle "needed new blood".[\[51\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-ut-2018feb13-51) Chipotle stock went up $30.27, or 12.04%, as a result of the announcement. However, other analysts criticized the announcement by saying that "the move goes against everything the burrito chain stands for".[\[52\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-cnbc-2018feb14-52)

In May 2018, Chipotle announced that it would relocate its headquarters from Denver to [Newport Beach, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newport_Beach,_California "Newport Beach, California"). Corporate functions handled in their Denver and New York offices would move to Newport Beach or to an existing office in [Columbus, Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbus,_Ohio "Columbus, Ohio"). This move would impact 400 workers, some being offered relocation and retention packages.[\[53\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-53)

In May 2018, Chipotle announced the "Chipotlane" and began testing it in the U.S. The Chipotlane acts as a drive-thru, but it is for mobile order pickup only; customers are unable to order food at the handout window but are welcome to pick up their mobile orders through the Chipotlane or inside the restaurant. As of 2023, there are over 500+ locations, which include the Chipotlane in order to make the restaurant run more efficiently.[\[54\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-54)

In June 2018, the company announced the closing of 65 under-performing restaurants.[\[55\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-55)[\[56\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-56)

Ells broke all ties with the company in March 2020 by resigning as its chairman and departing from its board of directors.[\[57\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-dp-2020mar06-57)

In January 2023, the company announced plans to hire 15,000 workers and expand to 7,000 locations, up from a previous goal of 6,000. This would double its footprint. At the time of the announcement, it had more than 100,000 employees.[\[58\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-58)[\[59\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-59) An article in _[The Wall Street Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Wall_Street_Journal "The Wall Street Journal")_ in July noted the chain would focus on opening 700–800 new locations in small towns and cities as part of its plan to expand by nearly 3,800 locations in territory traditionally dominated by chains like [McDonald's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonald's "McDonald's") and [Applebee's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Applebee's "Applebee's").[\[60\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-60)

In 2023 Chipotle saw $9.9 billion in revenue with 14.3% growth year on year and $3 million in average unit volumes (per restaurant revenue) and 7.9% growth in comparable sales; in 2023, 37.4% of their revenue came via online orders, third-party delivery services or their new Chipotlanes.[\[25\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-:0-25)

In September 2024, Chipotle announced it was collaborating with Vebu, a company that develops automation and robotics equipment for the food industry,[\[61\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-61) on a new automated avocado processing machine, Autocado. The companies claimed that this machine could peel and cut an avocado in less than 30 seconds and operate unattended on a large batch, which could significantly reduce the time and labor required to prepare large quantities of guacamole.[\[62\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-62)

Chipotle's share price fell more than 9% after fiscal second quarter results showed same-store sales shrinking by 4%, compared to 0.4% in the prior quarter.[\[63\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-63)

In April 2025, Chipotle announced the opening of a location in Mexico by early 2026, looking to expand into new markets in the region.[\[64\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-64)

In August 2025, Chipotle announced plans of delivering food via drone aircraft within the greater Dallas area through a partnership with [Zipline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zipline_(drone_delivery_company) "Zipline (drone delivery company)").[\[65\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-65)

In July 2026, Chipotle opened its first restaurant in Mexico in [San Pedro Garza Garcia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Pedro_Garza_Garc%C3%ADa "San Pedro Garza García"), [Nuevo Leon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuevo_Le%C3%B3n "Nuevo León"), in partnership with [Alsea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alsea_(company) "Alsea (company)"). The company planned additional restaurants in Nuevo Leon during 2026 and an expansion into Mexico City in 2027.[\[66\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-66)

In August 2026, Chipotle opened its first restaurant in Saudi Arabia, in [Riyadh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riyadh "Riyadh"), through a franchise partnership with [Alshaya Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alshaya_Group "Alshaya Group").[\[67\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-67)

In September 2026, Chipotle opened its first restaurant in South Korea, in the [Gangnam District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gangnam_District "Gangnam District"), via a joint venture with [SPC Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SPC_Group "SPC Group").[\[68\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-68)Korean media reported long queues at the Gangnam restaurant from its opening day, with a line of more than 100 meters forming before the noon opening and an average wait of about three hours. S&C Restaurants Holdings, the joint venture that operates Chipotle in South Korea, said it planned to open a second and third restaurant by the end of 2026, all directly operated, and Korean media reported that the second would be inside the Shinsegae Department Store’s Gangnam branch.[\[69\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-69)

### Expansion attempts into other types of cuisines

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=2 "Edit section: Expansion attempts into other types of cuisines")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/ShopHouse_lunch_bowl.jpg/250px-ShopHouse_lunch_bowl.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:ShopHouse_lunch_bowl.jpg) A [ShopHouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ShopHouse_Southeast_Asian_Kitchen "ShopHouse Southeast Asian Kitchen") lunch bowl

In the 2010s, Ells tried to diversify the company by creating or acquiring several subsidiaries. These subsidiaries offered very different cuisines compared to their parent company. Examples include soul food ( [Soul Daddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soul_Daddy "Soul Daddy")), Asian cuisine ( [ShopHouse Southeast Asian Kitchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ShopHouse_Southeast_Asian_Kitchen "ShopHouse Southeast Asian Kitchen")), pizza (Pizzeria Locale), and burgers (Tasty Made). In 2023, Chipotle had also tried incorporating [California-inspired bowls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_cuisine "California cuisine") into the Farmesa Fresh Eatery concept. The few test locations were not a financial success, and all concepts were discontinued.

In 2011, Steve Ells was a judge for the TV show _[America's Next Great Restaurant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/America's_Next_Great_Restaurant "America's Next Great Restaurant")_ and investor of [ANGR Holdings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soul_Daddy "Soul Daddy"), the company that would launch the winning concept's restaurants. Chipotle agreed to purchase Ells' investment in ANGR at his cost, provide support for ANGR operations, and invest a total of $2.3million in cash contributions.[\[70\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-fnd-70) The winning concept, Soul Daddy, was quickly closed after operating for eight weeks.[\[71\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-MOACLOSURE-71)

In September 2011, Chipotle opened an Asian fast-casual concept restaurant named [ShopHouse Southeast Asian Kitchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ShopHouse_Southeast_Asian_Kitchen "ShopHouse Southeast Asian Kitchen") in Washington, D.C.[\[72\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-shophouse-72) The company said the new restaurant "would follow the Chipotle service format and its focus on 'food with integrity' in ingredients".[\[73\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-Jennings-73) Chipotle's plan was to start with only one store and see how the restaurant worked out before expanding the concept.[\[74\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-Anderson-74)

On July 29, 2016, the company announced the opening of its first [Tasty Made](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tasty_Made "Tasty Made") burger restaurant in the fall.[\[75\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-lat-75)

The newer restaurant concepts did not perform as well as they had hoped, so ShopHouse Southeast Asian Kitchen and Tasty Made were closed in March 2017[\[76\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-eater-2017mar09-76) and February 2018[\[77\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-eater-2018mar01-77) respectively, leaving only Pizzeria Locale operating besides the parent company.

In February 2023, Chipotle opened their first Farmesa Fresh Eatery in Santa Monica, California, serving bowls containing customers' choice of a protein, a green or grain, two sides, a topping, and a selection of up to five sauces. Some of the choices included steak, salmon, whipped potatoes, sweet potato chips, beets, and cauliflower.[\[78\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-pintado-78)[\[79\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-79)

In July 2023, Chipotle exited the highly competitive pizza restaurant industry by announcing that they were closing all of their remaining Pizzeria Locale locations and would "dissolve the business".[\[80\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-usatoday-2023jul06-80) Chipotle also abandoned Farmesa in April 2024.[\[81\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-cnbc-2024apr24-81)

### Ownership

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=5 "Edit section: Ownership")\]

Chipotle is mainly owned by institutional investors, who hold around 89% of all shares. The 10 largest shareholders in late 2024 were:[\[84\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-84)

- [The Vanguard Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Vanguard_Group "The Vanguard Group") (8.97%)
- [BlackRock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BlackRock "BlackRock") (8.14%)
- [State Street Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Street_Corporation "State Street Corporation") (4.26%)
- [T. Rowe Price](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T._Rowe_Price "T. Rowe Price") (3.92%)
- [JPMorgan Chase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JPMorgan_Chase "JPMorgan Chase") (3.31%)
- [Capital World Investors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_World_Investors "Capital World Investors") (2.93%)
- [Fidelity Investments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fidelity_Investments "Fidelity Investments") (2.49%)
- [AllianceBernstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AllianceBernstein "AllianceBernstein") (2.23%)
- [Geode Capital Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geode_Capital_Management "Geode Capital Management") (2.11%)
- [Pershing Square Capital Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pershing_Square_Capital_Management "Pershing Square Capital Management") (1.83%)

### Corporate management

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=6 "Edit section: Corporate management")\]

Chipotle's team includes a residing corporate office of managers and its board of directors. Members of both teams are appointed to serve on committees: audit, compensation, and nominating and corporate governance.

By late 2016, the top management team consisted of the chief executive officer, Steve Ells; the chief financial officer, Jack R. Hartung; the chief marketing and development officer, Mark Crumpacker.[\[85\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-Chipotle:_Investor_Relations-85) At that time, the board of directors consists of: Ells, Patrick Flynn, Albert Baldocchi, Neil Flanzraich, Darlene Friedman, Stephen Gillet, [Kimbal Musk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimbal_Musk "Kimbal Musk") and John Charlesworth.[\[86\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-Chipotle:_Board_of_Directors-86) On March 14, 2018, it was reported that Mark Crumpacker, who had previously been charged in a 2016 cocaine ring indictment, would be leaving the company.[\[87\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-87)

Ells was [chairman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chairman "Chairman") of the company, and was [chief executive officer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_executive_officer "Chief executive officer") until November 2017.[\[88\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-88)[\[48\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-ReplaceFounder-48) He had a 1.25% stake in the company in 2010.[\[89\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-reklaitis-89) The labor-market research firm Glassdoor reported that Ells earned $29million in 2014, versus a median of $19,000 for Chipotle's workers, making the CEO-to-worker pay ratio 1522:1.[\[90\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-90)

On February 13, 2018, Chipotle announced that Taco Bell CEO [Brian Niccol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Niccol "Brian Niccol") would replace Ells as CEO starting on March 5 while Ells would retain his chairman position.[\[50\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-nyt-2018feb13-50)

On March 6, 2020, Ells resigned as chairman and left the board of directors, breaking his final ties to the company. At the same time, Niccol was appointed chairman, and the size of the board was reduced from ten to seven directors.[\[91\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-ut-2020mar06-91)[\[57\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-dp-2020mar06-57)

On August 13, 2024, [Starbucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starbucks "Starbucks") announced it was hiring Niccol as its next CEO.[\[92\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-92) Chief Operating Officer Scott Boatwright was appointed by the board as interim CEO effective August 31, 2024.[\[93\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-93) At the same board meeting, company director Scott Maw was named chairman of the board and retiring CFO Jack Hartung was named president.[\[94\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-94) Four months later, Boatwright was named permanent CEO by the board in November 2024.[\[95\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-95)

Chipotle's key management as of January 2026 consists of:[\[96\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-96)

- Scott Boatwright, Chief Executive Officer and Board member
- Adam Rymer, Chief Financial Officer
- Curt Garner, President, Chief Strategy and Technology Officer
- Ilene Eskenazi, Chief Legal and Human Resources Officer
- Jason Kidd, Chief Operating Officer
- Laurie Schalow, Chief Corporate Affairs Officer
- Kerry Bridges, Chief Food Safety Officer

#### Operation and distribution

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=7 "Edit section: Operation and distribution")\]

All of Chipotle's restaurants are company-owned, rather than [franchised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franchising "Franchising").[\[97\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-tampa-97)As of December2012[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit), 1,430 restaurants have since opened throughout the United States and Canada, with locations in 43 states, Ontario, British Columbia, and the District of Columbia.[\[98\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-10klocations-98)[\[99\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-99)

The field team are the employees who work closely with, but not directly within, specific restaurants. The field support system includes apprentice team leaders (step up from restaurateurs), team leaders or area managers, team directors, and regional directors (not atypical for them to oversee more than fifty locations).[\[100\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-100) Because Chipotle does not franchise, all restaurants are owned and operated directly by the corporation itself. Thus, whenever Chipotle is in the process of launching a new location, the field team hires a new general manager and trains them at a current location so that they will be ready for the new location when it opens for business. The corporate office takes care of finding and funding new locations as well.[\[101\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-101)

In July 2023, the company announced it was testing a robot developed to cut the time to prepare avocados for guacamole in half amid an ongoing labor shortage. It was one of multiple chains incorporating automation into its operations at the time.[\[102\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-102)

## International

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=8 "Edit section: International")\]

Chipotle had 17 locations outside of the United States by October 2014, with the majority in Canada, and the UK was in the process of opening more locations.[\[103\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-tmf-2014oct17-103) The rate of overseas expansion was slower than expected.[\[104\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-tgam-2010aug16-104) Many of the press reviewers thought that the food was overpriced for their area.[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-lexpress-2012may31-7)[\[105\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-Genuss-2013oct14-105)

In July 2023, the company announced it would partner with the Kuwait-based [Alshaya Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alshaya_Group "Alshaya Group") in its first-ever franchise deal to expand into the Middle East.[\[106\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-106)

In April 2025, Chipotle announced it had signed a joint venture with [Alsea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alsea_(company) "Alsea (company)") to open restaurants in [Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico "Mexico") by early 2026, and possibly in other areas in which Alsea operates in Latin America.[\[107\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-107)

In September 2025, Chipotle announced it signed a joint venture with [SPC Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SPC_Group "SPC Group") to open restaurants in eastern Asia, with the first Chipotle restaurants opening in South Korea and Singapore in 2026. At the time of the announcement, Chipotle's existing international portfolio of restaurants included 60 locations in Canada, 20 in the United Kingdom, six in France, two in Germany, three in Kuwait and three in the UAE.[\[108\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-108)

### North America

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=9 "Edit section: North America")\]

### Canada

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=10 "Edit section: Canada")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/ChipotleMexicanGrillMarkham3.jpg/250px-ChipotleMexicanGrillMarkham3.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:ChipotleMexicanGrillMarkham3.jpg) Chipotle Mexican Grill in Markham, Ontario, Canada

In August 2008, Chipotle opened its first location outside of the United States in [Toronto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toronto "Toronto"), Ontario, Canada.[\[32\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-ts-2008aug13-32) A second Toronto store opened in 2010.[\[104\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-tgam-2010aug16-104) The first Canadian location outside Toronto opened in Vancouver in December 2012.[\[109\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-biv-2012dec13-109) Chipotle has six locations in the Vancouver-area, including Vancouver, West Vancouver, North Vancouver, Burnaby, Port Coquitlam, Langley and Surrey.[\[110\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-awesom-110) In 2023, Chipotle announced that seventh store would open up in the Vancouver region.[\[111\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-111) The first location in the country's Capital opened in February 2017 at the [Rideau Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rideau_Centre "Rideau Centre").[\[112\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-cn-2017feb02-112)

The first location in Alberta was opened in Calgary in October 2023.[\[113\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-113) The first Chipotle restaurant in Edmonton opened in September 2024.[\[114\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-114)

Across Canada, there are 26 stores in Ontario, 16 in British Columbia, and 2 in Alberta.[\[115\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-115)

### Europe

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=11 "Edit section: Europe")\]

#### United Kingdom

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=12 "Edit section: United Kingdom")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ad/Chipotle_London_Baker_St.jpg/250px-Chipotle_London_Baker_St.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Chipotle_London_Baker_St.jpg) The second Chipotle Mexican Grill location in London, located on [Baker Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baker_Street "Baker Street")

Chipotle expanded to Europe with the first European restaurant opening in May 2010 in London.[\[116\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-london-116)[\[117\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-kaplan-117)[\[118\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-ti-2010jun05-118) A second location opened in London in September 2011.[\[119\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-bighospitality-2011sep02-119) The following year, three additional locations quickly opened in the London area.[\[120\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-bighospitality-2012mar13-120) After this growth spurt, the rate of further expansion in London slowed greatly with the sixth location appearing in 2013[\[121\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-bighospitality-2012sep20-121) and the seventh in June 2015.[\[122\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-bighospitality-2015apr13-122) Although Chipotle blames the slow growth in the United Kingdom on the British unfamiliarity with Mexican foods,[\[123\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-bn-2013feb26-123) several locally owned burrito chains had opened locations across the United Kingdom during the same interval.[\[124\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-bn-2013apr23-124)As of February 2024[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit), there were 19 UK locations, of which 17 were in London.[\[125\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-125)\[ _[bettersourceneeded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Questionable_sources "Wikipedia:Verifiability")_\]

#### France

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=13 "Edit section: France")\]

The first location in France opened in Paris in May 2012.[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-lexpress-2012may31-7)[\[126\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-10q-126) Expansion in France was much slower than that in the United Kingdom or Canada, with a second location in Paris opening in 2013[\[127\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-fastandfood-2013oct15-127) and a third location in 2014.[\[128\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-lexpress-2014dec27-128) At the time of its opening in 2014, the restaurant at La Défense was the largest Chipotle location in the world at 7,000 square feet (650m2), while a typical Chipotle restaurant is usually between 2,200 square feet (200m2) and 2,500 square feet (230m2).[\[129\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-mm-2014mar05-129) A fourth Parisian location was opened in [Levallois-Perret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levallois-Perret "Levallois-Perret") in 2015[\[130\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-lcn-2015sep16-130) followed by a fifth[\[131\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-lb-2016sep14-131) Parisian location in [Saint-Germain-des-Prés](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint-Germain-des-Pr%C3%A9s "Saint-Germain-des-Prés") in 2016. The company opened its seventh location in France and its first location outside the Paris region, inside [Lyon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyon "Lyon")'s [La Part-Dieu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Part-Dieu_(shopping_mall) "La Part-Dieu (shopping mall)") shopping mall in 2021.[\[132\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-132)[\[133\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-133)

#### Germany

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=14 "Edit section: Germany")\]

The first location in Germany opened up in [Frankfurt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankfurt "Frankfurt")'s [Skyline Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skyline_Plaza_(Frankfurt) "Skyline Plaza (Frankfurt)") shopping mall in August 2013.[\[134\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-Genuss-2013jun06-134)[\[105\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-Genuss-2013oct14-105) A second location opened in Frankfurt's [MyZeil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MyZeil "MyZeil") shopping mall in April 2019.[\[135\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-135)

### Asia

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=15 "Edit section: Asia")\]

#### Kuwait

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=16 "Edit section: Kuwait")\]

Franchise partner Alshaya Group opened its first Chipotle restaurant in the city state of Kuwait inside [The Avenues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Avenues_(Kuwait) "The Avenues (Kuwait)") mall in April 2024.[\[136\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-136)

#### United Arab Emirates

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=17 "Edit section: United Arab Emirates")\]

Franchise partner Alshaya Group opened its first Chipotle restaurant in the United Arab Emirates in Dubai at the [Jumeirah Beach Residence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jumeirah_Beach_Residence "Jumeirah Beach Residence") in September 2024.[\[137\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-137) Alshaya opened its first location in [Abu Dhabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dhabi "Abu Dhabi") at the [Yas Mall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yas_Mall "Yas Mall"), the third in the UAE, in August 2025.[\[138\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-138)

#### Qatar

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=18 "Edit section: Qatar")\]

Franchise partner Alshaya Group opened its first Chipotle restaurant in [Doha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doha "Doha"), the capital of Qatar, in September 2025 at the [Villaggio Mall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villaggio_Mall "Villaggio Mall").[\[139\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-139)[\[140\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-140)

#### South Korea

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=19 "Edit section: South Korea")\]

The first location in South Korea opened in [Gangnam District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gangnam_District "Gangnam District") in September 2026.[\[141\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-141)

#### Riyadh

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=20 "Edit section: Riyadh")\]

Franchise partner Alshaya Group opened its first Chipotle restaurant in Riyadh, the capital of Saudi Arabia, in August 2026.[\[142\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-142)

### Mexico

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=21 "Edit section: Mexico")\]

The first location in Mexico opened in [San Pedro Garza Garcia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Pedro_Garza_Garc%C3%ADa "San Pedro Garza García"), [Nuevo Leon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuevo_Le%C3%B3n "Nuevo León"), in partnership with Alsea in July 2026.[\[143\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-143)

## Menu

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=22 "Edit section: Menu")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Chipotle_Brandon.jpeg/250px-Chipotle_Brandon.jpeg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Chipotle_Brandon.jpeg) A Chipotle restaurant in Brandon, Florida, having the typical service-line layout with menu above

Chipotle's menu consists of five items: [burritos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burrito "Burrito"), bowls, [tacos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taco "Taco"), [quesadillas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quesadilla "Quesadilla"), and [salads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salad "Salad"). The price of each item is based on the choice of chicken, pork [carnitas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnitas "Carnitas"), [barbacoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbacoa "Barbacoa"), steak, tofu-based "sofritas",[\[144\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-144)[\[145\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-145) or vegetarian (with guacamole or queso, which would be at an extra charge otherwise). Additional optional toppings are offered free of charge, including: rice, beans, four types of [salsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salsa_(sauce) "Salsa (sauce)"), fajita vegetables, sour cream, cheese, and lettuce.[\[146\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-146)[\[147\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-ingred-147) Although Chipotle styles itself as a "Mexican Grill", its menu is more accurately characterized as Cal-Mex, a form of [Mexican-American cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican-American_cuisine "Mexican-American cuisine") with its roots in California.

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/Chipotle_Chips_and_Queso.jpg/250px-Chipotle_Chips_and_Queso.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Chipotle_Chips_and_Queso.jpg) Chipotle regular sized chips and queso with a side of sour cream

When asked in 2007 about expanding the menu, Steve Ells said, "\[I\]t's important to keep the menu focused, because if you just do a few things, you can ensure that you do them better than anybody else."[\[148\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-integ-148) Chipotle also offers a children's menu.[\[149\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-149)[\[150\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-150) Some restaurants sell [beer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beer "Beer") and [margaritas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margarita "Margarita") in addition to [soft drinks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soft_drink "Soft drink") and fruit drinks.[\[151\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-151)

The majority of food is prepared in each restaurant. Some exceptions are the beans and carnitas, which are prepared at a central kitchen in Chicago, Illinois.[\[152\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-nyt-152) None of the restaurants have [freezers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refrigerator "Refrigerator"), [microwave ovens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microwave_oven "Microwave oven"), or [can openers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Can_opener "Can opener").[\[153\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-153)

The chain experimented with breakfast foods at two airports in the Washington (D.C.) metropolitan area but decided against expanding the menu in that direction.[\[154\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-154)[\[155\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-155)[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-bi-2015apr10-156) Starting in 2009, selected restaurants had offered a [pozole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pozole "Pozole") soup,[\[157\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-157)[\[158\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-westword-2009apr16-158)[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-ccb-2011jan11-159) which has since been discontinued. Starting in 2009, Chipotle tested a vegan chicken product (made by [Gardein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gardein "Gardein")) called "Garden Blend" in various cities but discontinued it in 2010.[\[160\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-160)

In 2013, Chipotle began testing the tofu-based "sofritas" option (made by Hodo Soy) in Northern California restaurants; following its successful trial, it was rolled out nation-wide in 2014.[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-161)[\[162\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-162)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/ChipotleBurritoBowl.jpg/250px-ChipotleBurritoBowl.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:ChipotleBurritoBowl.jpg) A chicken and barbacoa burrito bowl with guacamole

In June 2015, Chipotle began test marketing a pork and chicken [chorizo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chorizo "Chorizo")-type sausage as a new protein option at selected locations in the Kansas City area.[\[163\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-fnc-2015jun04-163)[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-eater-2015jun02-164)[\[165\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-kcs-2015jun02-165) Some food writers have expressed their health related concerns over the protein's relatively high sodium content since a 4-ounce (110g) serving contains 293 calories and 803 milligrams of sodium[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-kcs-2015jun03-166) while the [American Heart Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Heart_Association "American Heart Association")'s recommended daily amount is less than 1,500 milligrams of sodium.[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-american_heart_assn-167) In contrast, the protein options with next highest sodium contents are Barbacoa with 530 milligrams and sofritas with 555 milligrams.[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-kcs-2015jun03-166) An earlier version of the Mexican sausage was tested in Denver and New York City in 2011,[\[168\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-hp-2011jun29-168) but that test was terminated when that version of the sausage was perceived as looking too greasy.[\[169\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-ct-2015jun02-169) Chorizo was discontinued in September 2017[\[170\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-170) but was returned to the menu in the following year for a limited time.[\[171\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-171)

In July 2020, Chipotle began test marketing a cauliflower rice option at 55 locations in Colorado and Wisconsin.[\[172\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-172)

In August 2021, Chipotle began testing a company-made vegan chorizo made with pea protein.[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-173) The vegan chorizo (now called plant-based chorizo) was subsequently released nation-wide as a limited-time only item on January 3, 2022,[\[174\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-174) before being removed on March 4, 2022.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

Chipotle accepts [fax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fax "Fax") orders, and in 2005 the company added the ability to order online from their website. For both online and fax orders, customers proceed to the front of the line to pay for pre-ordered food.[\[175\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-175) In 2009, Chipotle released an [app](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/App_Store_(iOS) "App Store (iOS)") for the [iPhone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPhone "IPhone") that allows users to find nearby Chipotle locations, place an order, and prepay with a credit card.[\[176\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-176) In 2013, Chipotle released an [Android](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Google_Play "Google Play") app that allows users to locate nearby Chipotle locations, place an order, prepay with a credit or gift card, and access favorites and recent orders.[\[177\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-androidandme1-177)[\[178\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-androidpolice1-178)

In late 2021, the company added a non-food item to the menu—cilantro soap. According to published reports, the soap sold out the day after its release. The soap was produced after an August 2021 Instagram post of what was thought to be a mock photo of the product. It was an effort to engage fans and haters of the herb that, "plays into a larger trend of turning digital moments into real life experiences," according to Chris Brandt, Chipotle's Chief Marketing Officer.[\[179\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-179) Chipotle uses green marketing and its "Food with Integrity" platform to boost its brand image.[\[180\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-180)

### Nutrition

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=23 "Edit section: Nutrition")\]

In 2003, a [Center for Science in the Public Interest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Science_in_the_Public_Interest "Center for Science in the Public Interest") report stated that Chipotle's burritos contain over 1,000 calories, which is nearly equivalent to two meals' worth of food.[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-181)[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-182) When a burrito with carnitas, rice, vegetables, cheese, guacamole, and salsa was compared with a typical [Big Mac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Mac "Big Mac"), the burrito had more fat, cholesterol, carbohydrates, and sodium than the Big Mac, but it also had more protein and fiber.[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-183) The restaurant has also received praise—Health.com included the restaurant in its list of the "Healthiest Fast Food Restaurants".[\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-184)

Chipotle's vegetarian options include rice, black beans, fajita vegetables (onions and bell peppers), salsa, guacamole, queso and cheese.[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-special-185) All items other than the meats, cheese, sour cream, queso and honey vinaigrette dressing are [vegan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veganism "Veganism").[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-special-185) As of late 2013, Chipotle developed a new cooking strategy for the [pinto beans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinto_bean "Pinto bean"), eliminating the bacon and making them vegetarian and vegan-friendly.[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-186) The cheese is processed with vegetable-based [rennet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rennet "Rennet") in order to be suitable for vegetarians.[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-special-185) In April 2010, Chipotle began testing a vegan "Garden Blend" option, which is a plant-based [meat alternative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meat_analogue "Meat analogue") marinated in chipotle [adobo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adobo "Adobo"), at six locations in the U.S.[\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-187)[\[188\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-188) The flour tortillas used for the burritos and soft tacos are the only items that contain gluten.[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-special-185)

### Portion size irregularities

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=24 "Edit section: Portion size irregularities")\]

Some customers took to the social media platform [TikTok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TikTok "TikTok") accusing Chipotle of reducing portion sizes. In June 2024, a [Wells Fargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wells_Fargo "Wells Fargo") analyst and his team set investigated the matter and ordered 75 identically configured items from 8 different Chipotle locations in [New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City "New York City"). The team weighed each bowl and reported that portion sizes varied widely, ranging from 14 to 27 ounces (400 to 770g) with a median weight of 21.5 ounces (610g).[\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-189)

### Food sourcing

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=25 "Edit section: Food sourcing")\]

In 1999, while looking for ways to improve the taste of the carnitas,[\[17\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-parrish-17) founder Steve Ells was prompted by an article written by [Edward Behr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Behr_(food_writer) "Edward Behr (food writer)") to visit [Concentrated Animal Feeding Operations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concentrated_Animal_Feeding_Operation "Concentrated Animal Feeding Operation") (CAFOs).[\[190\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-190) Ells found the CAFOs "horrific", and began sourcing from open-range pork suppliers. This caused an increase in both the price and the sales of the carnitas burritos.[\[117\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-kaplan-117)

In 2001, Chipotle released a [mission statement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_statement "Mission statement") called _Food With Integrity_, which highlighted Chipotle's efforts to increase their use of naturally raised meat, [organic produce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organic_food "Organic food"), and dairy without added [hormones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hormone "Hormone").[\[4\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-barnes-4)

Ells testified before the [United States Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Congress "United States Congress") in support of the Preservation of Antibiotics for Medical Treatment Act, which was aimed to reduce the amount of antibiotics given to farm animals.[\[117\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-kaplan-117)[\[191\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-191)

From 2006 to 2012, the [Coalition of Immokalee Workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalition_of_Immokalee_Workers "Coalition of Immokalee Workers") (CIW), a Floridian farmworker organization, protested Chipotle's refusal to sign a Fair Food agreement, which would commit the restaurant chain to pay a penny-per-pound premium on its Florida tomatoes to boost tomato harvesters' wages, and to only buy Florida tomatoes from growers who comply with the Fair Food Code of Conduct.[\[192\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-192) In 2009, the creators of the documentary film _[Food, Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food,_Inc. "Food, Inc.")_ (along with 31 other leaders in the [sustainable food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sustainable_food_systems "Sustainable food systems") movement) signed an open letter of support for the CIW's campaign, stating that, "If Chipotle is sincere in its wishes to reform its supply chain, the time has come to work with the Coalition of Immokalee Workers as a true partner in the protection of farmworkers rights."[\[193\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-193) In September 2009, Chipotle announced that it would sidestep partnership with the CIW and instead work directly with East Coast Growers and Packers to increase wages for its tomato pickers.[\[194\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-194) Ells framed the dispute as a fundamental issue of control, stating that, "the CIW wants us to sign a contract that would let them control Chipotle's decisions regarding food in the future".[\[195\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-195) In October 2012, Chipotle signed an agreement with the CIW to improve working conditions.[\[196\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-196)

In January 2015, Chipotle pulled carnitas from its menu in a third of its restaurants; company officials cited [animal welfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animal_welfare "Animal welfare") problems at one of the suppliers, found during a regular audit, as the reason.[\[197\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-ferdman-197) Subsequently, a false rumor spread online claiming it was done to appease Muslims who consider pork to be unclean, leading to some protests on social media.[\[198\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-198) The company still uses antibiotic-free and hormone-free steak in its restaurants, despite being briefly forced to "serve beef that is not naturally raised" during the summer of 2013, posting an in-store notice each time that occurred.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-199) Roberto Ferdman of _[The Washington Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Washington_Post "The Washington Post")_ opined that Chipotle's stated mission to sell "food with integrity" may be "untenable" if meat producers continue to breach Chipotle's ethical standards.[\[197\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-ferdman-197)

Also in 2015, Chipotle stopped using [genetically modified](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genetically_modified_organism "Genetically modified organism") corn and soybeans in their foods, claiming to be the first nationwide restaurant to cook completely GMO free.[\[200\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-200) However, in 2019, Chipotle paid $6.5million to settle a lawsuit where the plaintiffs claimed that the company's food "may have been sourced from livestock that consumed GMO animal feed".[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-201)

In 2017, Chipotle committed to transitioning away from birds bred to maximize growth at the expense of animal well-being. However, in 2024, investigations by animal welfare groups showed that they were failing to meet these standards.[\[202\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-202)

## Food safety

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=26 "Edit section: Food safety")\]

In its annual reports, Chipotle has acknowledged that it "may be at a higher risk for food-borne illness outbreaks than some competitors due to our use of fresh produce and meats rather than frozen, and our reliance on employees cooking with traditional methods rather than automation".[\[203\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-203) Following the 2015 outbreaks, some food safety experts and media commentators argued that the variety of pathogens involved pointed to systemic problems with the company's food handling practices.[\[204\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-miller-204)[\[205\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-fivethirtyeight-2015dec10-205) On December 10, 2015, CEO Steve Ells released a statement apologizing for the outbreaks and promised changes to minimize future risks.[\[206\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-206) Chipotle's official sustainability reports details their environmental goals and sourcing practices.[\[207\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-207)

### Food poisoning outbreaks

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=27 "Edit section: Food poisoning outbreaks")\]

#### March 2008 hepatitis

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=28 "Edit section: March 2008 hepatitis")\]

In March and April 2008, the Community Epidemiology Branch of the San Diego County Health and Human Services Agency traced a [hepatitis A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hepatitis_A "Hepatitis A") outbreak in [San Diego County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Diego_County,_California "San Diego County, California") to a single Chipotle restaurant located in [La Mesa, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Mesa,_California "La Mesa, California"), in which 22 customers were infected with the virus.[\[208\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-sdc-2008may05-208)[\[209\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-kgtv-2008jun18-209)

#### April 2008 norovirus

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=29 "Edit section: April 2008 norovirus")\]

In 2008, Chipotle was implicated in a [norovirus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norovirus "Norovirus") outbreak in [Kent, Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kent,_Ohio "Kent, Ohio"), where over 400 people became ill after eating at a Chipotle restaurant.[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-210) Officials at the [Ohio Department of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_Department_of_Health "Ohio Department of Health") said that the outbreak was caused by _Norovirus Genotype G2_.[\[211\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-woio-2008apr28-211) Many of the victims were students at [Kent State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kent_State_University "Kent State University").[\[212\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-abj-2008apr18-212) The initial source of the outbreak was never found.

#### July 2015 _E. coli_

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=30 "Edit section: July 2015 E. coli")\]

In early November 2015, _[The Oregonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Oregonian "The Oregonian")_ reported that there was a little-known _E.coli_ outbreak that had occurred earlier in July in which five people were infected with the [O157:H7 strain of _E.coli_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Escherichia_coli_O157:H7 "Escherichia coli O157:H7"). The outbreak was traced to a single Chipotle location in Seattle and that the incident was not publicized at that time.[\[213\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-to-2015nov10-213)[\[214\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-st-2015nov05-214) Seattle public health officials defended their actions at that time by saying that the outbreak was over by the time they made an association with Chipotle. Health officials were unable to trace the source of the July outbreak and said that the cause of the July outbreak is unrelated to the October/November outbreak.[\[213\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-to-2015nov10-213)

#### August 2015 norovirus

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=31 "Edit section: August 2015 norovirus")\]

A norovirus outbreak occurred in August 2015 at a [Simi Valley, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simi_Valley,_California "Simi Valley, California"), location, ultimately affecting more than 200 people.[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-lat-2015sep04-215)[\[216\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-nyt-2016jan06-216) [Ventura County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ventura_County,_California "Ventura County, California") health inspectors found health violations during inspections following the outbreak.[\[217\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-217) In January 2016, a [federal grand jury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_grand_jury "Federal grand jury") issued a subpoena as part of a criminal investigation into the outbreak.[\[218\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-lat-2016jan06-218)

#### August 2015 _Salmonella_

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=32 "Edit section: August 2015 Salmonella")\]

At almost the same time as the Simi Valley norovirus outbreak, Minnesota health officials confirmed a _[Salmonella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salmonella "Salmonella")_ outbreak that affected 17 Minneapolis-area Chipotle restaurants in mid-August 2015. The source of the outbreak was traced back to contaminated tomatoes that were grown in Mexico.[\[219\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-mst-2015sep11-219)[\[220\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-mst-2015sep16-220) The Minnesota Department of Health reported that samples from 45 victims were tested and found that their illness was caused by the _Salmonella_ Newport bacterium as determined by [DNA profiling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DNA_profiling "DNA profiling").[\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-mdoh-2015sep10-221) Later, the state officials reported that the total of persons who became infected was increased to 64 and the number Chipotle locations in which they had acquired the bacterium was increased to 22, all located within the state of Minnesota.[\[222\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-mdoh-2015sep16-222)

#### October 2015 _E. coli_

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=33 "Edit section: October 2015 E. coli")\]

In October 2015, an outbreak of [Shiga toxin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiga_toxin "Shiga toxin")-producing _[Escherichia coli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Escherichia_coli "Escherichia coli")_ (STEC O26) was linked to several Chipotle locations in Washington and Oregon.[\[223\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-nn-2015oct31-223)[\[224\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-st-2015oct31-224) As a precaution, Chipotle closed 43 stores in Washington and Oregon pending the results and recommendations of health authorities. The restaurants reopened on November 11 after disposing of all food and deep cleaning the facilities.[\[225\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-nbc-2015nov11-225) A separate, smaller outbreak involving five people at Chipotle restaurants in Kansas and Oklahoma in late November was later determined to be caused by a genetically different strain of STEC O26.[\[226\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-cfdcap-2016feb01-226)

On February 1, 2016, the [Centers for Disease Control and Prevention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centers_for_Disease_Control_and_Prevention "Centers for Disease Control and Prevention") (CDC) closed their investigation, reporting that 55 persons in 11 states were infected during the main outbreak, with 21 requiring hospitalization and no fatalities.[\[226\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-cfdcap-2016feb01-226) Health authorities were unable to determine a specific source for the contamination.

#### December 2015 norovirus

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=34 "Edit section: December 2015 norovirus")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill_in_Cleveland_Circle%2C_December_2015.JPG/250px-Chipotle_Mexican_Grill_in_Cleveland_Circle%2C_December_2015.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Chipotle_Mexican_Grill_in_Cleveland_Circle,_December_2015.JPG) The closed restaurant on December 16, 2015

In December 2015, a norovirus outbreak at a Chipotle restaurant near [Boston College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_College "Boston College") sickened approximately 140 people, many of them students.[\[227\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-bg-2015dec10-227) The [Boston Public Health Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Public_Health_Commission "Boston Public Health Commission") traced the outbreak to a sick employee who had been allowed to work.[\[228\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-wbur-2015dec10-228)

In response to the 2015 outbreaks, Chipotle closed all locations on February 8, 2016, for an all-staff food safety meeting and hired a new head of food safety who instituted enhanced protocols including more frequent handwashing, produce sanitation procedures, and [Pascalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pascalization "Pascalization") to pre-treat ingredients.[\[229\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-229)[\[230\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-strom20160922-230)

#### July 2017 norovirus

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=35 "Edit section: July 2017 norovirus")\]

Despite corrective actions, the company faced another setback in implementing their safe food policies in July 2017. A norovirus outbreak is being investigated in Virginia. More than 130 people reported having norovirus-like symptoms and two individuals had tested positive for the virus after eating at a Chipotle restaurant in [Sterling, Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sterling,_Virginia "Sterling, Virginia"). The Loudoun County Health Department confirmed the illnesses from July 13–16, 2017. Shares of Chipotle stock dropped more than 10% on this news.[\[231\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-231)[\[232\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-232) On July 25, several news agencies reported that Chipotle officials confirmed that the "recent norovirus outbreak in Virginia was the result of lax sick policy enforcement by store managers" and that the company believed that an employee was the cause of the outbreak.[\[233\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-cnbc-2017jul25-233)[\[234\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-cnn-2017jul26-234)

#### July 2018 _Clostridium perfringens_

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=36 "Edit section: July 2018 Clostridium perfringens")\]

In late July 2018, Ohio public health officials launched an investigation after receiving 350 complaints from customers after they had eaten at a [Powell, Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powell,_Ohio "Powell, Ohio"), location.[\[235\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-235) By mid-August, the U.S. [Centers for Disease Control and Prevention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centers_for_Disease_Control_and_Prevention "Centers for Disease Control and Prevention") identified the bacteria that cause the outbreak which had affected over 700 people as _[Clostridium perfringens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clostridium_perfringens "Clostridium perfringens")_.[\[236\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-delish-2018aug20-236)[\[237\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-cnbc-2018aug16-237) This food borne outbreak has been called the worst incident to date that can be traced to a single restaurant location.[\[236\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-delish-2018aug20-236)[\[238\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-refinery29-2018aug08-238)

## Data breach

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=37 "Edit section: Data breach")\]

### 2017

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=38 "Edit section: 2017")\]

In April 2017, Chipotle first announced that their [point of sale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Point_of_sale "Point of sale") payment system was compromised during a three-week period from late March to mid-April but gave very little information about the incident.[\[239\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-fm-2017apr25-239)[\[240\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-sm-2017apr26-240) The following month, the company later revealed that 2,250 restaurants in 47 states were involved which could potentially affect hundreds of thousands of customers who may had their credit or debit card account information with security codes
