stolen.[\[241\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-eater-2017may30-241)[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-mrp-2017may29-242)[\[243\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-243)[\[244\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-securityintelligence-2017jun01-244) A few restaurants in Canada were also affected.[\[245\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-tt-2017may27-245) Some security experts criticized the way Chipotle had downplayed the incident.[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-mrp-2017may29-242) During the same month, a credit union in New Hampshire filed a class action lawsuit on the behalf of banks and credit unions against Chipotle for failure to provide elementary credit card data security in the most recent [data breach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_breach "Data breach") after a similar data breach that had occurred in 2004.[\[246\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-kmgh-tv-2017may19-246) In July 2017, [Gainesville, Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gainesville,_Florida "Gainesville, Florida"), police reported that an unidentified person took more than $17,000 from an [ATM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automated_teller_machine "Automated teller machine") at a local credit union by stealing from 40 different accounts that can be traced directly to the data breach.[\[247\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-osb-2017jul20-247)[\[248\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-sm-2017jul21-248)

### 2019

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=39 "Edit section: 2019")\]

A number of Chipotle mobile ordering app customers claimed that their mobile ordering app accounts had been hacked and reported fraudulent orders charged to their credit cards. Chipotle claimed that the customers were using unsecured passwords, and that any fraudulent orders were a result of this. The customers then argued that the passwords they were using were secure, and that their complaints involved an issue with the server.[\[249\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-249)[\[250\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-250)

## Lawsuits

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=40 "Edit section: Lawsuits")\]

In November 2016, three men filed a [class-action lawsuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Class-action_lawsuit "Class-action lawsuit") against Chipotle alleging that a burrito was listed as containing only 300 calories, when in fact it contained more. They are seeking "unspecified damages and for an injunction against the company to prevent it from posting what it calls misleading information on its food".[\[251\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-lawsuit1-251)[\[252\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-lawsuit2-252)[\[253\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-lawsuit3-253)

In January 2020, the company was fined $1.3million by the state of Massachusetts for 13,000 [child labor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_labour "Child labour") violations.[\[254\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-254)[\[255\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-255)[\[256\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-256)

## Labor issues and unionization

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=41 "Edit section: Labor issues and unionization")\]

In May 2019, Chipotle was in the news for having dismissed a manager in [St. Paul, Minnesota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Paul,_Minnesota "Saint Paul, Minnesota"), who had been [falsely accused](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_accusation "False accusation") of [racism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racism "Racism").[\[257\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-257)

In December 2019, the office of the general counsel of the [National Labor Relations Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Labor_Relations_Board "National Labor Relations Board") (NLRB), [Peter Robb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_B._Robb "Peter B. Robb"), filed a complaint against Chipotle, accusing the company of firing a worker in New York City in retaliation for trying to organize a union, as well as alleging that a manager in Manhattan threatened to fire workers if they engaged in protected union activities, implying they could even face physical violence as a result.[\[258\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-258)

In July 2022, Chipotle closed a store in [Augusta, Maine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augusta,_Maine "Augusta, Maine"), whose staff was trying to unionize, ostensibly due to staffing difficulties.[\[259\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-259) In November 2022, the NLRB issued a complaint against Chipotle, accusing the company of illegally closing the Augusta store and [blackballing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blacklist_(employment) "Blacklist (employment)") the employees at the store in violation of the [National Labor Relations Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Labor_Relations_Act_of_1935 "National Labor Relations Act of 1935").[\[260\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-260)

In August 2022, a restaurant in [Lansing, Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lansing,_Michigan "Lansing, Michigan"), became the chain's first location to vote to unionize with the [International Brotherhood of Teamsters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Brotherhood_of_Teamsters "International Brotherhood of Teamsters").[\[261\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-261)

At multiple points in 2022, various outlets highlighted Chipotle as an example of a restaurant chain investing in robotics and automation via Chippy, an autonomous kitchen assistant made by [Miso Robotics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miso_Robotics "Miso Robotics").[\[262\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-262)[\[263\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-263)

## Advertising and publicity

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=42 "Edit section: Advertising and publicity")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Chipotle_free_burrito_day.jpg/250px-Chipotle_free_burrito_day.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Chipotle_free_burrito_day.jpg) People line up outside a Chipotle in [East Lansing, Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Lansing,_Michigan "East Lansing, Michigan"), during a "Free Burrito Day" in 2006

In the past, Chipotle mainly relied on billboards, radio advertisements, and [word of mouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Word_of_mouth "Word of mouth") to advertise.[\[28\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-buzz-28) In 2010, the company initiated an ad campaign that mocks advice given to Chipotle by advertising agencies.[\[264\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-264) In 2012, Chipotle aired its first nationally televised commercial during the [54th Annual Grammy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/54th_Annual_Grammy_Awards "54th Annual Grammy Awards") ceremony.[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-265) Chipotle has run many promotions giving out free food to potential customers, especially when opening a new store. Stores also give out free burritos on certain holidays; for instance, on [Halloween](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halloween "Halloween"), some locations have had promotions in which free burritos are given to people who come dressed as a burrito.[\[266\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-266)

Chipotle gave away free burritos to reporters during the 1997 trial of [Timothy McVeigh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timothy_McVeigh "Timothy McVeigh"), which took place in Chipotle's hometown of Denver.[\[267\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-267) In addition, stores offered free burritos to those displaced by [Hurricane Katrina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Katrina "Hurricane Katrina").[\[268\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-268) Chipotle received attention when [Ozzy Osbourne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ozzy_Osbourne "Ozzy Osbourne")'s reality show _[The Osbournes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Osbournes "The Osbournes")_ featured the company's burritos heavily.[\[26\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-rocky2-feeding-26)

For Halloween 2010, Chipotle announced that customers dressed as a processed food product would receive a burrito for $2. The event was part of a $1million fundraiser for _[Jamie Oliver's Food Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamie_Oliver's_Food_Revolution "Jamie Oliver's Food Revolution")_ called "Boorito 2010: The Horrors of Processed Food".[\[269\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-269) Also in support of family farms, Chipotle released music videos of [Karen O](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_O "Karen O") of the [Yeah Yeah Yeahs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeah_Yeah_Yeahs "Yeah Yeah Yeahs") and [Willie Nelson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willie_Nelson "Willie Nelson").[\[270\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-270)

On September 12, 2013, Chipotle released an animated short called _[The Scarecrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Scarecrow_(2013_film) "The Scarecrow (2013 film)")_, with a companion mobile video game; both feature a narrative heavily critical of [industrial farming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intensive_farming "Intensive farming"), but little in the way of direct marketing for the chain.[\[271\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-271) The short features a cover of " [Pure Imagination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pure_Imagination "Pure Imagination")" from _[Willy Wonka & the Chocolate Factory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willy_Wonka_&_the_Chocolate_Factory "Willy Wonka & the Chocolate Factory")_, sung by [Fiona Apple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiona_Apple "Fiona Apple").[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-272) It was named one of the worst advertisements of 2013 by [The Wall Street Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Wall_Street_Journal "The Wall Street Journal").[\[273\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-273)

In 2011, Chipotle created the "Farm Team", which is a rewards program available only by invitation from restaurant managers. The Farm Team members have access to a special Chipotle website, where members can earn rewards, i.e. free food and T-shirts. The site offers members to, "learn where Chipotle's food comes from, take quizzes and polls, play games and watch videos about the company".[\[274\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-274) In April 2014, the Farm Team program was shut down.[\[275\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-275)

Chipotle sponsors Team Garmin-Barracuda (formerly Team Garmin-Chipotle, Team Garmin-Slipstream, Team Garmin-Transitions and Team Garmin-Cervélo) of the [International Cycling Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Cycliste_Internationale "Union Cycliste Internationale"),[\[276\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-velo-276)[\[277\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-277) and is an official team partner of the [Boston Celtics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Celtics "Boston Celtics"),[\[278\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-278) and the [Boston Bruins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Bruins "Boston Bruins").[\[279\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-279) In June 2009, Chipotle sponsored free screenings of _[Food, Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food,_Inc. "Food, Inc.")_, a film that criticizes the corporate food industry. Founder Steve Ells stated that he hoped the film would make customers appreciate Chipotle's _Food With Integrity_ policy.[\[280\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-280) From May until September 2009, Chipotle ran a contest on mychipotle.com, a [microsite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microsite "Microsite") which had a competition for the best user-created audio and video presentations about different combinations of ingredients.[\[281\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-281)[\[282\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-282) In July 2010, Chipotle began a campaign to support healthier lunch alternatives for students, in which money will be donated to The Lunch Box program based on how many spam Emails consumers forward to a company Email address.[\[283\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-283)

In 2014, Chipotle collaborated on a [viral marketing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viral_marketing "Viral marketing") campaign with the rock band [Spoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spoon "Spoon"), in support of the band's album, [They Want My Soul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/They_Want_My_Soul "They Want My Soul").[\[284\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-284)

For Chipotle's 18th anniversary, the company began wrapping its burritos in gold foil as part of a larger promotion to draw attention to its _Food With Integrity_ mantra.[\[285\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-285) Also as part of the gold foil campaign, Chipotle is offering prizes for customer-created pictures of items wrapped in gold foil. Chipotle hired comedian [Amy Sedaris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amy_Sedaris "Amy Sedaris") to create a comedic how-to video on wrapping with gold foil, and spread the video using [Twitter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter "Twitter").[\[286\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-286) In March 2013, Chipotle pulled its sponsorship of a [Boy Scouts of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boy_Scouts_of_America "Boy Scouts of America") event, citing that organization's [ban on LGBT members](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boy_Scouts_of_America_membership_controversies "Boy Scouts of America membership controversies").[\[287\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-287)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Chipotle_Cultivating_Thought.jpg/250px-Chipotle_Cultivating_Thought.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Chipotle_Cultivating_Thought.jpg) Cup and bag from the "Cultivating Thought" series, featuring the work of [Paulo Coelho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulo_Coelho "Paulo Coelho") and [Augusten Burroughs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augusten_Burroughs "Augusten Burroughs")

On March 14, 2016, a [National Labor Relations Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Labor_Relations_Board "National Labor Relations Board") (NLRB) administrative law judge concluded that Chipotle's [social media policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_media_policy "Social media policy"), and more specifically its application towards an employee who posted tweets regarding wages and working conditions, violated the [National Labor Relations Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Labor_Relations_Act "National Labor Relations Act") (NLRA).[\[288\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-288) A former Chipotle employee in [Havertown, Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Havertown,_Pennsylvania "Havertown, Pennsylvania"), wrote a series of tweets from his personal account about hourly workers being required to work on snow days. Chipotle instructed the employee to delete the tweets. The NLRB judge determined that Chipotle's request to delete the tweets violated the NLRA even though the employee was not disciplined as a result of his tweets.[\[289\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-289)

In July 2021, Chipotle rolled out limited edition gold foil at participating locations to celebrate American athletes competing in Tokyo at the [2020 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Summer_Olympics "2020 Summer Olympics").[\[290\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-290) On October 28, 2021, Chipotle announced $1million of burritos to be given away for free for the first 30,000 people to play the Chipotle experience on the video game _[Roblox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roblox "Roblox")_. The site ended up experiencing a three-day outage, but the company clarified that it was not the fault of Chipotle.[\[291\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-RobloxForbes-291)

On February 16, 2023, [MoneyGram Haas F1 Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haas_F1_Team "Haas F1 Team") announced a sponsorship deal with Chipotle. The Chipotle logo will be featured on both the nose and side of the [VF-23](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haas_VF-23 "Haas VF-23"), race-suits of drivers [Kevin Magnussen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Magnussen "Kevin Magnussen") and [Nico Hulkenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nico_H%C3%BClkenberg "Nico Hülkenberg") and team apparel.[\[292\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-292)

## Architecture

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=43 "Edit section: Architecture")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/2008-10-05_Chipotle_Mexican_Grill_in_Durham_new.jpg/250px-2008-10-05_Chipotle_Mexican_Grill_in_Durham_new.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:2008-10-05_Chipotle_Mexican_Grill_in_Durham_new.jpg) The exterior of a Chipotle in [Durham, North Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durham,_North_Carolina "Durham, North Carolina")[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/Chipotle_in_Chinatown_-_Washington%2C_DC.jpg/250px-Chipotle_in_Chinatown_-_Washington%2C_DC.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Chipotle_in_Chinatown_-_Washington,_DC.jpg) A Chipotle restaurant located outside [Capital One Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_One_Arena "Capital One Arena") in the Chinatown area of [Washington, D.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington,_D.C. "Washington, D.C.") Note the Chinese characters on the sign.

Architecturally, all Chipotle restaurants are built using most of the same material finishes (plywood, corrugated metal, stainless steel, exposed ductwork), although each store is unique.[\[17\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-parrish-17)[\[293\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-Ells-293) The interiors have been described as having an "industrial, sheet metal look".[\[294\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-294) Chipotle has built restaurants using white ceramic tile instead of stainless steel.[\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-295) It costs the company approximately $850,000 to open a new restaurant.[\[296\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-296) When the first Chipotle opened, Steve Ells asked his friend, sculptor Bruce Gueswel, to design the chairs and a styled Mayan king whose face was loosely based on that of [civil rights movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_rights_movement "Civil rights movement") leader [Martin Luther King Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Luther_King_Jr. "Martin Luther King Jr."), a personal inspiration of Gueswel, for the restaurant. Both items were made from wood and metal. Gueswel has continued to design and build the art and chairs for all subsequent Chipotle restaurants.[\[297\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-297)

Most Chipotle locations display a photograph of the original restaurant, which is near the [University of Denver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Denver "University of Denver") campus on Evans Avenue.[\[17\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-parrish-17) Instead of a photograph of itself, the original location has a photograph of the Dolly Madison Ice Cream that previously occupied the location. In 2010, Chipotle began opening smaller concept locations that have lower costs of development and occupancy.[\[298\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-298)

Chipotle has made arrangements to add [solar panels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_panel "Solar panel") to 75 of its restaurants.[\[299\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-pundit-299)

Chipotle was the defendant in a lawsuit for failure to comply with the [Americans with Disabilities Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Americans_with_Disabilities_Act_of_1990 "Americans with Disabilities Act of 1990") (ADA). [Maurizio Antoninetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurizio_Antoninetti "Maurizio Antoninetti"), a customer who used a wheelchair, claimed he was denied the "Chipotle Experience", because he was unable to see the food preparation. The case against the company was upheld in the [United States Court of Appeals for the Ninth Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Court_of_Appeals_for_the_Ninth_Circuit "United States Court of Appeals for the Ninth Circuit"),[\[300\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-ADA-300) and the [Supreme Court of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_the_United_States "Supreme Court of the United States") declined to hear Chipotle's appeal, leaving the Ninth Circuit's ruling intact.[\[301\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-301) Chipotle has "an official disability policy of bringing ingredients to the tables of diners with disabilities and doing tableside preparation." Chipotle is retrofitting restaurants affected by the ruling, replacing the walls in front of the food preparation area with lower ones or transparent panels. They are incorporating the new design elements into new restaurants.[\[300\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-ADA-300) The case was one of over twenty ADA-related lawsuits filed by Antoninetti, who died in 2011.[\[300\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-ADA-300)[\[302\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-302)

## Other restaurant units

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chipotle_Mexican_Grill&action=edit&section=44 "Edit section: Other restaurant units")\]

Former units[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/5.2.11SoulDaddyPPSandwichByLuigiNovi2.jpg/250px-5.2.11SoulDaddyPPSandwichByLuigiNovi2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:5.2.11SoulDaddyPPSandwichByLuigiNovi2.jpg) A typical meal at [Soul Daddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soul_Daddy "Soul Daddy")

- Farmesa (2023–2024) was a restaurant concept specializing in custom-made bowls. The first location opened in Santa Monica in March 2023, but Chipotle abandoned the concept in April 2024.[\[78\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-pintado-78)[\[81\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-cnbc-2024apr24-81)

- Pizzeria Locale (2013–2023) was a fast casual pizza chain formed as a partnership with the original Pizzeria Locale restaurant in [Boulder, Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boulder,_Colorado "Boulder, Colorado").[\[303\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-dp-2013dec18-303) The chain grew to seven locations by 2017, but contracted after closing locations outside Colorado in 2018.[\[304\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-eater-2018jun27-304) Chipotle closed the remaining locations and dissolved the business in July 2023.[\[80\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-usatoday-2023jul06-80) The original Boulder restaurant, which was not owned by Chipotle, was renamed Pizzeria Alberico.[\[305\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-305)

- [Soul Daddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soul_Daddy "Soul Daddy") (May–June 2011) was a [soul food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soul_food "Soul food") fast casual restaurant managed by Chipotle. It operated three locations for less than five weeks before closing.[\[70\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-fnd-70)[\[71\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-MOACLOSURE-71)

- [ShopHouse Southeast Asian Kitchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ShopHouse_Southeast_Asian_Kitchen "ShopHouse Southeast Asian Kitchen") (2011–2017) was an [Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_cuisine "Asian cuisine") fast-casual concept following the Chipotle service format.[\[72\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-shophouse-72) The chain grew to 15 locations before all were closed in March 2017.[\[76\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-eater-2017mar09-76)

- [Tasty Made](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tasty_Made "Tasty Made") (2016–2018) was a burger restaurant concept. The sole location in [Lancaster, Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lancaster,_Ohio "Lancaster, Ohio"), opened in October 2016 and closed in February 2018.[\[306\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipotle_Mexican_Grill#cite_note-cbf-2018mar01-306)
